`internal/accounting` is Vision §3's accounting/finance foundation: a firm-scoped chart of accounts and a double-entry journal - the minimal, generic backbone every other module (inventory valuation, purchasing, payroll, ...) eventually posts to, not a full accounting suite. Storage is `accounts`/`journal_entries`/`journal_lines` (`migrations/0009_financial_management_core.up.sql`) - firm-scoped, mandatory RLS (Rule 3), plus a `journal_template jsonb` column added to the existing `workflow_transitions` table for the workflow-to-ledger bridge below.

- **The one invariant**: a posted journal entry's debit lines and credit lines must sum to the same total. Enforced in `PostJournalEntryTx` (journal.go), application-layer, inside the same transaction as the entry's own insert - not a database `CHECK`/trigger, since a cross-row-and-cross-table sum constraint is impractical to express as a plain Postgres `CHECK` and a trigger-based equivalent would duplicate logic this function already owns as the single write path into `journal_entries`/`journal_lines`. The check itself is computed by Postgres's own exact `numeric` arithmetic (`SUM(amount) FILTER (WHERE side = 'debit') - SUM(amount) FILTER (WHERE side = 'credit')`, cast to `text` and checked for "is this string all zeros" - `isZeroDecimal`) - never a Go float, anywhere in this package, for any total. `PostJournalEntryTx` is the one shared entry point every caller that can post a journal entry goes through - `PostJournalEntry` (the owner-gated manual-entry HTTP path) and `internal/workflow.ExecuteTransition` (the bridge, below) - the same "one shared Tx-level function, every caller goes through it" pattern `internal/workflow.DefineWorkflowTx` already established for workflow provisioning.
- **Chart-of-accounts seeding** (`seed.go`'s `SeedDefaultChartOfAccountsTx`): called once from inside `internal/wizard.CreateDefaultFirm`'s own transaction, parametrically from the wizard's existing `SeedSelection` answers (`Sells`/`PurchasesFromSuppliers` - not manufacturing, since the wizard's manufacturing branch is still a `NodePlaceholder` dead end with no answer to key off). Core accounts (Cash, Trade Receivables, Trade Payables, Owner Equity, Retained Earnings) are seeded for every firm; Inventory/Sales Revenue/Cost of Goods Sold when `Sells`; Inventory (again)/Accounts Payable/Purchase Expense when `PurchasesFromSuppliers`. **Inventory is deliberately seeded by both groups**, deduplicated by account code before insert (a firm answering yes to both would otherwise hit `accounts`' `UNIQUE (firm_id, code)` constraint) - a firm that purchases from suppliers without also selling products (e.g. buying materials/supplies, not resale goods) still needs somewhere to post received goods; without this, `purchase_order`'s `receive` transition (below) would hit `ErrAccountNotFound` the first time it ran for such a firm.
- **The workflow-to-ledger bridge** (`internal/workflow/spec.go`'s `TransitionSpec.Journal *JournalTemplate`): an OPTIONAL journal template a transition can carry - `Description` (`"{{field}}"` placeholders, substituted from the transition's merged instance payload) plus `Lines []LineTemplate{AccountCode, Side, AmountField}`. `AmountField` names one payload field, or two joined by `"*"` (e.g. `"quantity*unit_price"`) for the one payload-derived computation this bridge supports natively - the realistic shape of a sale/purchase amount. At `ExecuteTransition` time (engine.go), the template is resolved against the merged payload and posted via `PostJournalEntryTx` **inside the same transaction** as the state change itself - both commit together or neither does. If the named field(s) are simply absent from the payload, the whole template is skipped for that call (not an error) - deliberately non-breaking (Rule 6 in spirit) for any `ExecuteTransition` caller that predates a transition gaining a `JournalTemplate`, or that just doesn't supply the financially-relevant fields on a given call. A field that IS present but malformed (wrong type), or a posting failure once fields are present (unknown account code - structurally unreachable for an unbalanced-entry failure here, since this bridge always emits balanced lines by construction), fails the whole transition and rolls back the state change with it.
  - `stock_to_sale`'s `record_sale` transition is wired with a real example: DR Trade Receivables / CR Sales Revenue, `quantity*unit_price` (`unit_price` is this batch's addition to a payload that already conventionally carried `quantity`; both stay OPTIONAL fields, not required, so every pre-existing `record_sale` caller that never supplied them keeps working exactly as before, just without a posted entry).
  - `purchase_order`'s **`receive`** transition (Sent → Received) is wired the same way: DR Inventory / CR Trade Payables, `total_amount` - the point a real financial event actually happens (goods received, both a real asset and a real liability created at once - standard perpetual-inventory treatment for a purchase not yet paid). **`send`** (Draft → Sent) deliberately carries no `JournalTemplate`, contrary to an earlier draft of this design that proposed posting there too: sending a PO to a supplier is a commercial communication, not a completed transaction under accrual accounting (nothing has actually been received yet - posting there would expense before the matching receipt, violating the matching principle, and would double-count Trade Payables against `receive`'s own entry for the same purchase). `supplier_name`/`total_amount` are two new OPTIONAL fields on `PurchaseOrderSpec.Fields` (this workflow had no payload schema before this batch).
- **Manual journal entries**: `PostJournalEntry` (owner-gated) is `PostJournalEntryTx`'s pool-based, HTTP-reachable counterpart for a caller-authored entry, not derived from any workflow transition - `POST .../journal-entries`.
- **`GetAccountBalance`**: `SUM(debits) - SUM(credits)` or the reverse, depending on the account type's normal balance side (`AccountType.normalDebitBalance`, accounts.go) - computed by the same exact-arithmetic, cast-to-`text` convention as the balance check above.
- **Financial reports** (`reports.go`, computed views - no new tables, no stored report rows): **`GetProfitAndLoss`** sums every revenue account credit-minus-debit and every expense account debit-minus-credit over an optional `[from, to]` window (either bound may be nil), grouped by account, plus `NetIncome = TotalRevenue - TotalExpenses`. **`GetBalanceSheet`** sums asset accounts debit-minus-credit and liability/equity accounts credit-minus-debit, all up to an `asOf` instant. **`CurrentEarnings`** (net income accrued since the last close, folded into `TotalEquity`) exists because this batch has no period-closing mechanism - without folding unclosed revenue/expense into equity, `Assets = Liabilities + Equity` would show as permanently, structurally false the moment any firm posts its first sale (an asset increases with nothing on the liability/equity side), which would make the `Balanced` flag a permanent false alarm rather than a genuine signal. With `CurrentEarnings` folded in, `Balanced` staying `true` is the expected, correct state under normal operation - `Balanced: false` is then a real data-integrity signal, not an artifact of missing closing entries. Both totals and the balance check are computed via Postgres's own exact `numeric` arithmetic, same discipline as everywhere else in this package.
- **Authorization**: `IsMember` gates every read (`ListAccounts`, `GetAccountBalance`, `ListJournalEntries`, `GetProfitAndLoss`, `GetBalanceSheet`) - ordinary firm data visibility, the same tier as `internal/workflow.ListInstances`. `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates every account/manual-entry mutation (`CreateAccount`, `UpdateAccount`, `PostJournalEntry`) - a structural firm-financial decision, the same tier as `DefineWorkflowForFirm`/`CreateRole`. `PostJournalEntryTx` itself makes no authorization decision (`ciaudit:ignore-firmid-check`, same reasoning as `DefineWorkflowTx`) - its callers (`PostJournalEntry`, `ExecuteTransition`) have already checked before reaching it.

HTTP surface (`internal/accounting/handlers.go`):

- `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/accounts`, `PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/accounts/{accountID}` (code/type immutable once created - only name/isActive), `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/accounts/{accountID}/balance`
- `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/journal-entries` (`?limit=`/`?offset=` paging on the list, `X-Total-Count` header, same convention as the audit log/workflow-instances endpoints)
- `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/reports/pnl?from=&to=` (both optional, RFC3339), `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/reports/balance-sheet?as_of=` (optional RFC3339, defaults to now)

Frontend: `/settings/accounts` (owner-gated chart-of-accounts management, `components/Accounting/AccountsManager.tsx`), `/financials` (member-gated, three tabs - Account Balances, Profit & Loss with a from/to date range, Balance Sheet with an as-of date, both date pickers plain `<form method="get">`s driving URL search params, not client state, matching `WorkflowDefinitionView`'s own `?page=`/`?q=` convention), and a read-only journal-template section on the generic `/workflows/[key]` page for any transition that carries one. `components/Workflow/DefinitionBuilder.tsx` also gained a per-transition "Journal" expander (account code chosen from the firm's real chart of accounts via `/api/firms/{firmID}/accounts`, never free text) so an owner can attach a `JournalTemplate` to a transition they're defining through the UI, not just through Go-defined specs like `stock_to_sale`/`purchase_order`.

### Running the financial management core tests

`internal/accounting/accounting_integration_test.go` and `reports_integration_test.go` need a real Postgres, same convention as everywhere else above. `internal/workflow/journal_bridge_integration_test.go` and `purchase_order_journal_integration_test.go` cover the bridge itself (`record_sale`/`receive` posting, `send` posting nothing, a missing amount field skipping non-fatally).

```
export ZONARYOS_TEST_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos:zonaryos@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
make migrate
go test ./internal/accounting/... -v
go test ./internal/workflow/... -run "RecordSale|PurchaseOrderReceive|SeedDefaultChartOfAccounts" -v
```

## Payload validation framework (workflow field validators)

`internal/workflow.CreateInstance`'s payload validation (the per-`FieldSpec` checks described above under "Workflow engine") was refactored from a single switch statement over `FieldType` into a small validator-registry pipeline - a structural refactor only, no behavior change: every existing test passes unchanged, and the same `ErrPayloadValidation` errors are produced for the same bad inputs as before.

- **`FieldValidator` interface** (`validation.go`): `Validate(value any, spec FieldSpec, vctx ValidationContext) error`. `ValidationContext{Ctx, Pool, FirmID, Tx}` carries whatever a validator needs to check a value against live firm data (e.g. the `reference`/`person`/`product`/`supplier`/`delivery`/`customer` validators, which look up a referenced row by ID inside the same transaction `CreateInstance` is already running in) - `Tx` may be `nil` for a validator invoked outside a transaction, though nothing in this codebase does that today.
- **The registry**: `var fieldValidators = map[FieldType]FieldValidator{...}`, one entry per `FieldType` (`string`/`number`/`boolean`/`date`/`enum`/`array`/`reference`/`person`/`product`/`supplier`/`delivery`/`customer`), each a small struct implementing `FieldValidator` - the scalar types (`string`/`number`/`boolean`/`date`) share one `scalarValidator{FieldType}` implementation and `checkScalarValue`, exactly as the old switch's scalar branches did. `RegisterFieldValidator(ft FieldType, v FieldValidator)` is exported so a caller (a future module, or a test) can register a validator for a new `FieldType` without editing `validation.go` itself.
- **`validatePayload`** now loops over a definition's `[]FieldSpec`, looking up each field's validator in the registry and calling `Validate` - an unregistered `FieldType` fails the same way an unhandled switch case used to (`ErrPayloadValidation`, "unknown type"), and a missing required field is still rejected before any validator runs (a validator is never called for an absent field).
- **Testing a new validator without touching engine.go**: `internal/workflow/validation_test.go`'s `mockFieldValidator` demonstrates the extension point directly - it registers a mock validator for a scratch `FieldType` via `RegisterFieldValidator` (cleaned up with `t.Cleanup`), then calls `validatePayload` and asserts the mock was invoked with the expected value/spec, that its returned error propagates, and that it's never called for an absent-and-required field. This is a pure unit test (no Postgres) - `ValidationContext.Tx` is passed as `nil`, exercised only by the mock, not by any real validator.

## Multi-currency foundation

`internal/currency` is the address/tax/multi-currency batch's manual-entry exchange rate mechanism (Vision §3) - explicitly a foundation, not real-time rate integration: rates are entered by a platform admin, not fetched from any external rate API (out of scope for this batch, same as VAT/GST calculation and fiscal reporting - see the "Address and tax framework" section below for those same boundaries).

- **Storage**: `exchange_rates` (`migrations/0020_validation_currency_localization.up.sql`) - `from_currency`/`to_currency`/`rate numeric(19,8)`/`valid_from`/`valid_to`/`source`. Deliberately **not** firm-scoped and carries no RLS policy: an exchange rate is a platform-wide fact (USD→TRY is the same number for every firm), not tenant data - the one exception to Never-Violate Rule 3 in this codebase, and an intentional one, not an oversight. `exchange_rates_pair_valid` is a `UNIQUE (from_currency, to_currency, valid_from)` index, so the same rate source can't double-insert an identical (pair, effective-date) row.
- **`GetRateTx`/`GetRate`** resolve the rate for `(from, to)` effective at a given instant: the row with the latest `valid_from <= at` (and `valid_to IS NULL OR valid_to > at`) for that pair. **Identity short-circuit**: `from == to` returns `"1"` without querying the table at all - a currency always converts to itself at parity, and this guarantees `ConvertAmount` never fails merely because nobody bothered to insert a same-currency rate row. No matching row for a non-identity pair returns `ErrRateNotFound`, a sentinel every caller (below) checks specifically.
- **`ConvertAmountTx`/`ConvertAmount`** multiply an amount by the resolved rate via one exact Postgres `numeric` cast (`multiplyDecimal`, `SELECT ($1::numeric * $2::numeric)::text`) - never Go floating point, the same discipline `internal/accounting`'s balance arithmetic follows. The pool-based `GetRate`/`ConvertAmount` and Tx-based `GetRateTx`/`ConvertAmountTx` pairing mirrors `internal/accounting.PostJournalEntry`/`PostJournalEntryTx`'s own "pool wrapper for standalone callers, Tx primitive for callers already inside a transaction" convention.
- **HTTP surface**: `GET /api/exchange-rates?from=&to=&at=` is deliberately unauthenticated (`mux.HandleFunc`, not wrapped in the Keycloak-verifying `auth(...)` middleware) - exchange rates carry no sensitive or tenant-scoped data, and the currency-conversion UI needs to read them without a session. `POST /api/exchange-rates` is platform-admin-only (`internal/platformadmin.Allowlist`, the same allowlist-then-404 gate every other platform-admin endpoint uses) - only ZonaryOS operators enter rates, not firm owners.
- **The journal bridge wire-in, and its safety guarantee**: `internal/accounting.PostJournalEntryTx` now converts a `LineInput`'s amount when its `Currency` differs from the resolved account's own currency, using `ConvertAmountTx` at `time.Now()`. If `ConvertAmountTx` returns `ErrRateNotFound` specifically, the conversion is skipped - the entry posts the ORIGINAL, unconverted amount tagged with the account's own currency (a 1:1 fallback), and a `slog.Warn` operational log records the skipped conversion (`from`, `to`, `accountCode`) for an operator to notice and backfill a rate later. **Any other error still fails the transition** (propagated, not swallowed) - only the specific "no rate on file" case degrades gracefully; a real DB error, for instance, is never silently ignored. This is the deliberate design point requested for this batch: a missing exchange rate is an expected, recoverable gap (a platform admin simply hasn't entered that pair yet), not a reason to block a real business operation (a sale, a purchase, any transition with a `JournalTemplate`) from completing. `internal/workflow.ExecuteTransition`'s `resolveJournalLines` supplies each line's `Currency` from a new well-known OPTIONAL payload field, `"currency"` (`instanceCurrencyPayloadField`) - the same "well-known hardcoded payload field name" convention `customerIDPayloadField` already established; a payload that never sets `"currency"` produces lines with an empty `Currency`, which `PostJournalEntryTx` treats as "use the account's own currency" (no conversion attempted), so this wiring is fully backward-compatible with every `JournalTemplate` caller that predates it.
- Frontend: `/platform-admin/exchange-rates` (`components/PlatformAdmin/ExchangeRatesManager.tsx`) - a manual-entry form (from/to currency, rate, optional source) plus a rate list, gated the same way `/platform-admin` itself is (`notFound()` for a non-allowlisted caller, re-using `fetchPlatformAdminFirms` purely as the allowlist probe rather than adding a second one).

### Running the multi-currency tests

`internal/currency/currency_integration_test.go` (rate lookup with date filtering, identity-pair short-circuit, `ErrRateNotFound`, conversion math) and `internal/accounting/currency_bridge_integration_test.go` (`TestPostJournalEntry_MismatchedCurrencyAutoConverts`, `TestPostJournalEntry_MissingRateFallsBackTo1To1WithoutFailing`) need a real Postgres, same convention as everywhere else above.

## Address and tax framework (foundation)

`internal/localization` is Open Points item 24's foundation-only first step: a firm-scoped address book and a firm-scoped set of named tax rates, both plain CRUD - explicitly **not** VAT/GST calculation logic, and **not** fiscal/tax reporting (item 24 stays open for both; see `docs/OPEN_POINTS.md`).

- **Storage**: `addresses` and `tax_rates` (`migrations/0020_validation_currency_localization.up.sql`) - both firm-scoped with mandatory RLS `_tenant_isolation` policies (Rule 3), unlike `exchange_rates` above. `tax_rates.rate numeric(5,4)` is stored as a **fraction** (e.g. `0.2000` for 20%) - the migration's own SQL comment and `resolveLineTaxRate`'s doc comment both call this out, because `invoice_lines.tax_rate numeric(5,2)` (pre-existing, from the invoicing batch) stores a **percentage** (`20.00`); the one place these units meet is `resolveLineTaxRate` (below), which converts fraction→percentage via one exact Postgres `numeric` multiply (`rate * 100`), never Go float math.
- **`CreateAddress`/`ListAddresses`/`UpdateAddress`/`DeleteAddress`** and **`CreateTaxRate`/`ListTaxRates`/`UpdateTaxRate`/`DeleteTaxRate`**: `IsMember` gates reads, `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates writes - the same tier as `internal/accounting`'s chart-of-accounts mutations. `Update*` is a full-replace (every field required), not a partial patch, matching `internal/accounting.UpdateAccount`'s own precedent for firm-structural records with few enough fields that partial-patch complexity isn't worth it.
- **`GetTaxRateTx`** is exported specifically for `internal/invoicing` to call from within its own already-open, already-authorized transaction (`ciaudit:ignore-firmid-check` on both it and `resolveLineTaxRate`, same reasoning as `PostJournalEntryTx`'s own annotation: the caller has already checked before reaching them).
- **The invoice-line override**: `invoice_lines.tax_rate_id` (nullable, `ON DELETE SET NULL`, added by the same migration) is an OPTIONAL field on `InvoiceLineInput`. When set, `resolveLineTaxRate` (`internal/invoicing/invoices.go`) looks up the referenced `tax_rates` row and overrides the line's stored `tax_rate` with that rate's value (converted to a percentage, as above) instead of whatever plain `TaxRate` string the caller supplied; when unset, behavior is unchanged from before this batch (`TaxRate` string, `"0"` if empty). Resolved once, at write time - a `tax_rates` row's rate changing later, or the row being deleted, never retroactively changes an already-written invoice line (why the column is `ON DELETE SET NULL` rather than `RESTRICT`: deleting a tax rate that's been referenced by past invoices must stay possible). Wired into both invoice-creation paths that build lines (`insertInvoiceTx`, shared by `CreateInvoice` and the workflow bridge's `CreateInvoiceTx`) and the standalone `AddInvoiceLine`/`UpdateInvoiceLine`.
- **HTTP surface**: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/addresses`, `DELETE /api/firms/{firmID}/addresses/{addressID}`, `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/tax-rates`, `DELETE /api/firms/{firmID}/tax-rates/{taxRateID}`.
- Frontend: `/settings/addresses` (`components/Settings/AddressesManager.tsx`) and `/settings/tax-rates` (`components/Settings/TaxRatesManager.tsx`) - both owner-gated the same way `/settings/accounts` is (`isOwner`, inline "not authorized" message rather than `notFound()` - a firm-structural settings page, not a platform-admin one).

### Running the address/tax tests

`internal/localization/localization_integration_test.go` (`TestAddress_CRUD`, `TestTaxRate_CRUD`) and `internal/invoicing/taxrate_override_integration_test.go` (`TestAddInvoiceLine_TaxRateIDOverridesTaxRate`, `TestAddInvoiceLine_PlainTaxRateStillWorksWithoutTaxRateID`) need a real Postgres, same convention as everywhere else above.

## HR core, task/project management, and the reporting foundation

`internal/hr` is Vision §3's minimal HR foundation: every business has people, so a firm-scoped record of who they are (employees/contractors/partners, `people`) and what contract terms they're under (`contracts`, append-only history per person) - not a payroll/time-tracking/performance-review system (see `migrations/0010_hr_core.up.sql`'s own scope-boundary comment). `people.custom_fields` (jsonb) gives firm-specific HR attributes without a schema change, the same "PostgreSQL + JSONB hybrid" principle `firms.attributes` already uses. `IsMember` gates every read (`ListPeople`, `GetPerson`, `ListContracts`); `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates every write (`CreatePerson`, `UpdatePerson`, `CreateContract`) - the same tier as `internal/accounting`'s chart-of-accounts mutations. Every person/contract write records one `audit_log` entry, same pattern `internal/firm`'s own metadata edits use.

- HTTP surface: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/people`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/people/{personID}`, `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/people/{personID}/contracts`.
- Frontend: `/hr` (`components/HR/PeopleManager.tsx`) - a people list with inline-expandable contract history and an add-contract form; owner-gated create/edit controls (rendered conditionally, still backend-enforced regardless).

**Task/project management** connects the existing `task_approval` workflow (see the "Firm-creation wizard" section below) to HR: `internal/workflow/task_approval.go`'s `TaskApprovalSpec` gained one OPTIONAL payload field, `assignee_person_id`, of a NEW `FieldType` - `FieldTypePerson` (`spec.go`). Unlike `FieldTypeReference` (which resolves against another workflow definition's instances), `FieldTypePerson` resolves against `internal/hr`'s `people` table directly - `checkPersonField` (engine.go) calls `hr.PersonExistsTx`, the same "resolve a foreign reference inside the caller's own already-open transaction" shape `checkReferenceField` already uses, just crossing a module boundary (`internal/workflow` importing `internal/hr`, no cycle - `internal/hr` has no dependency back on `internal/workflow`) rather than staying within one. Left OPTIONAL (not required) for the same Rule 6 reason every other payload-field addition in this codebase is: an unassigned task remains fully valid, and every pre-existing `CreateInstance` call for this workflow keeps working unchanged.

- `/tasks` (`app/[locale]/tasks/page.tsx`) is this batch's first genuinely cross-module VIEW: it fetches `task_approval` instances (`internal/workflow`) and the firm's people (`internal/hr`) as two independent, independently-authorized reads, then joins them in the page itself on `payload.assignee_person_id` - there is no single backend query spanning both tables, and none was needed. Filterable by `?status=`/`?assignee=` (plain `<select>`s in a `<form method="get">`, URL-driven like `/financials`'s date pickers, not client state).
- `CreateInstanceForm.tsx` renders a `FieldTypePerson` field as a plain `<select>` (`people` prop, fetched server-side by `WorkflowDefinitionView.tsx` only when the definition actually has a person field) rather than a search-as-you-type picker like `FieldTypeReference`'s own `ReferencePicker` - HR rosters are small for any firm this batch's scope targets, so there's no need for a second paginated GET proxy route just to filter a short list.

**Reporting foundation**: `internal/reports` is Vision §3's KPI dashboard - the first package that imports all three of `internal/accounting`, `internal/workflow`, and `internal/hr` at once, still only ever reading through the same RLS-scoped, `IsMember`-gated path every other read in this system uses; no new table, no new data. Each KPI is a small declarative `KPIDescriptor` (`kpi.go`) - a stable `Key` (client-translated per Rule 4, never literal text from the backend) plus a `Kind` (`account_balance_now`, `account_balance_period`, `workflow_state_count`, or `person_count`) and whatever that `Kind` needs (account codes, a workflow key + states, a person status). The dashboard's actual five KPIs (`kpiDescriptors`) are all instances of these four `Kind`s, referencing `internal/accounting`'s own exported account-code constants and `internal/workflow.TaskApprovalKey` - not five hand-written SQL queries. Adding a KPI that fits an existing `Kind` is a one-line addition to `kpiDescriptors`, not a new code path; only a genuinely new shape of aggregation needs a new `Kind` and its own compute function. Every total is computed by Postgres's own exact `numeric` arithmetic (`accountBalanceSum`), same discipline as `internal/accounting`'s own reports; an account/workflow/person-status with no data yet for a firm reads back as a real `0`, not an error.

- HTTP surface: `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/reports/kpis`.
- Frontend: `/reports` (a simple tile grid, `t("kpi." + result.key)` for each label).

### Running these tests

`internal/hr/hr_integration_test.go` (people/contract CRUD, cross-firm isolation), `internal/workflow/task_approval_hr_integration_test.go` (the `FieldTypePerson` cross-module validation), and `internal/reports/kpi_integration_test.go` (KPI correctness across real seeded accounting/workflow/HR data, plus the empty-firm-reads-zeros case) all need a real Postgres:

```
export ZONARYOS_TEST_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos:zonaryos@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
make migrate
go test ./internal/hr/... -v
go test ./internal/workflow/... -run TaskApprovalAssignee -v
go test ./internal/reports/... -v
```

## Inventory management, warehouse stock, and supplier management

`internal/inventory` is the structural layer under the existing `stock_to_sale`/`purchase_order` workflows: real products (`products`), per-location on-hand quantities (`stock_levels`), an immutable movement log (`stock_movements`), and suppliers (`suppliers`) - not just a freeform `item` string in a workflow instance's payload (see `migrations/0011_inventory_core.up.sql`'s own scope-boundary comment for what stays deliberately out of scope: warehouse picking/packing, barcode scanning, multi-warehouse transfer UI, expiry/batch/lot tracking - the data model supports per-location stock via `stock_levels.location`, but no transfer workflow yet). `products.custom_fields`/`suppliers.custom_fields` (jsonb) give firm-specific attributes without a schema change, the same convention `people.custom_fields`/`firms.attributes` already use. `stock_movements` is append-only - `zonaryos_app` is granted `SELECT, INSERT` only, no `UPDATE`/`DELETE`, the same immutability principle `journal_entries`/`journal_lines` establish for the ledger: a movement is a historical fact, corrected by a new opposing movement, never rewritten. `IsMember` gates every read; `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates every product/supplier write - the same tier as `internal/accounting`'s chart-of-accounts mutations.

- HTTP surface: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/products`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/products/{productID}`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/products/{productID}/stock`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/stock-movements` (paginated), `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/suppliers`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/suppliers/{supplierID}`.
- Frontend: `/inventory` (`components/Inventory/InventoryManager.tsx`) - a product list with per-location stock levels, a low-stock highlight (client-side, mirroring the `lowStockProducts` KPI's own condition), and recent movement history; `/suppliers` (`components/Suppliers/SuppliersManager.tsx`) - supplier CRUD, the same create-form/row-action shape `PeopleManager.tsx` established.

**The workflow-to-inventory bridge** mirrors the financial management core's own workflow-to-ledger bridge exactly, not a new mechanism: `internal/workflow/spec.go`'s `TransitionSpec` gained an OPTIONAL `StockAdjustment *StockAdjustmentTemplate` field (a product field name, a quantity field name, a direction "increase"/"decrease", and a reason), the same declarative-template shape `Journal *JournalTemplate` already has. `ExecuteTransition` (`engine.go`) resolves it against the transition's merged instance payload and calls `internal/inventory.AdjustStockTx` - a `*Tx`-suffixed function taking an already-open `pgx.Tx`, performing no authorization of its own (the caller's `ExecuteTransition` has already checked the transition's own permission) - in the SAME transaction as the state change and any journal entry, so a stock change can never end up out of sync with the workflow instance that produced it. A missing product/quantity field is a skip, not an error (Rule 6 - every pre-existing `ExecuteTransition` call for `stock_to_sale`/`purchase_order` keeps working unchanged); a malformed present field, or `AdjustStockTx` itself returning `ErrInsufficientStock` (a sale that would take `stock_levels.quantity` below zero), fails the whole transition and rolls back the state change with it - the design brief's explicit "reject, not just warn". `AdjustStockTx` checks the resulting quantity itself, in Go, using Postgres's own exact `numeric` arithmetic, before writing - the real `CHECK (quantity >= 0)` constraint on `stock_levels.quantity` is the actual authoritative guarantee (defense in depth, the same "RLS is the real enforcement" reasoning this codebase applies everywhere), not merely relied upon after the fact. `stock_to_sale`'s `record_sale` transition carries `StockAdjustment{ProductField: "product_id", QuantityField: "quantity", Direction: "decrease", Reason: "sale"}`; `purchase_order`'s `receive` transition (not `send` - the same accounting-timing reasoning its own `Journal` template already documents) carries `StockAdjustment{..., Direction: "increase", Reason: "purchase"}`.

- `product_id`/`supplier_id` are two new payload fields, each of a NEW `FieldType` - `FieldTypeProduct`/`FieldTypeSupplier` (`spec.go`) - the same shape `FieldTypePerson` already established: resolves against one specific table (`products`/`suppliers`) directly via `checkProductField`/`checkSupplierField` (`engine.go`) calling `inventory.ProductExistsTx`/`inventory.SupplierExistsTx`. Both OPTIONAL on `stock_to_sale`/`purchase_order` for the same Rule 6 reason every payload-field addition in this codebase is.
- `CreateInstanceForm.tsx` renders `FieldTypeProduct`/`FieldTypeSupplier` fields as plain `<select>`s (`products`/`suppliers` props, fetched server-side by `WorkflowDefinitionView.tsx` only when the definition actually has such a field), the same reasoning `FieldTypePerson`'s own picker gives for not needing a search-as-you-type control.

**Inventory KPIs**: `internal/reports/kpi.go` gained a fifth `KPIKind`, `inventory_kpi`, dispatching further on an `InventoryMetric` (`low_stock_products`, `total_inventory_value`) - neither metric fits any of the existing four `Kind`s (they aggregate `products`/`stock_levels`, a shape none of account balances, workflow-state counts, or people counts share), so this is one new `Kind` covering inventory-specific aggregations, not two hand-written one-off queries. `low_stock_products` counts distinct products whose on-hand quantity (summed across every location, `LEFT JOIN` so a product with zero `stock_levels` rows still counts) is below their own `min_quantity` threshold (products with `min_quantity = 0`, the column's own documented default, are never flagged); `total_inventory_value` sums `quantity * cost_price` across every `stock_levels` row, rounded to `numeric(19,4)` to match every other currency figure in this codebase.

### Running these tests

`internal/inventory/inventory_integration_test.go` (product/supplier CRUD, `AdjustStockTx`'s decrease/increase/insufficient-stock-rejection behavior), `internal/workflow/stock_bridge_integration_test.go` (the `record_sale`/`purchase_order.receive` stock hooks end-to-end, `FieldTypeSupplier` validation), and `internal/reports/kpi_integration_test.go` (the two new inventory KPIs' correctness) all need a real Postgres:

```
export ZONARYOS_TEST_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos:zonaryos@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
make migrate
go test ./internal/inventory/... -v
go test ./internal/workflow/... -run 'RecordSale|PurchaseOrderReceiveIncreasesStock|PurchaseOrderRejectsUnknownSupplier' -v
go test ./internal/reports/... -run InventoryMetrics -v
```

## Logistics management, warehouse delivery tracking, and customer-facing data

`internal/logistics` and `internal/crm` are the structural layer under two existing workflows' own terminal states: `deliveries` gives the `stock_to_sale` workflow's sale a real, mutable shipment record instead of nothing at all, and `customers` gives the `customer_pipeline` workflow's `customer` state a real customer record instead of just a state label (`migrations/0013_logistics_crm_core.up.sql`). Unlike `stock_movements`/`journal_entries`, both tables are mutable (`zonaryos_app` is granted `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE`) - a delivery's status changes over its own lifecycle, and a customer's contact details get corrected, neither is a historical fact-log entry. `custom_fields` (jsonb) on both tables follows the same firm-specific-attribute convention `products.custom_fields`/`people.custom_fields` already established. `IsMember` gates every read; `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates every delivery/customer write - the same tier every other module in this codebase uses for its own core writes.

- HTTP surface: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/deliveries`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/deliveries/{deliveryID}`, `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/customers`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/customers/{customerID}`.
- Frontend: `/logistics` (`components/Logistics/DeliveriesManager.tsx`) - a delivery list with status/tracking, an owner-only inline status-change `<select>`, and a status filter, the same filter-form shape `/tasks` already established; `/customers` (`components/Customers/CustomersManager.tsx`) - customer CRUD, with pipeline-converted customers linking back to `/workflows/customer_pipeline` (a deliberate limitation, documented in the component itself: there is no per-instance detail route yet, so the link goes to the generic definition list rather than the one specific lead).

**The workflow-to-logistics/CRM bridge** is the same declarative-template mechanism the financial management core's workflow-to-ledger bridge and the inventory batch's workflow-to-stock bridge already established, extended to a third and fourth template rather than reinvented: `TransitionSpec` (`spec.go`) now carries four OPTIONAL template fields - `Journal`, `StockAdjustment`, `Delivery`, `Customer` - each resolved independently against the transition's merged instance payload inside `ExecuteTransition`, in the SAME transaction as the state change, so none of a sale's ledger entry, stock decrement, delivery record, or customer record can end up applied without the others. Each template's "required" field decides skip-vs-apply on its own: `DeliveryTemplate.DestinationAddressField` and `CustomerTemplate.NameField` are the one field whose absence from the payload means "this template doesn't apply to this instance" (Rule 6 - every pre-existing `record_sale`/`convert` call with no delivery/customer data keeps working exactly as before); every other field on each template (origin address, carrier, tracking number, email, phone) is optional and simply resolves to `NULL` if absent. A malformed *present* field, or the downstream `logistics.CreateDeliveryTx`/`crm.CreateCustomerTx` call itself failing, fails the whole transition and rolls back the state change with it, same as `StockAdjustment`'s `ErrInsufficientStock` handling. `stock_to_sale`'s `record_sale` transition carries `Delivery{DestinationAddressField: "destination_address", OriginAddressField: "origin_address", CarrierField: "carrier", TrackingNumberField: "tracking_number", ReferenceField: "item"}` alongside its existing `Journal`/`StockAdjustment`; `customer_pipeline`'s `convert` transition (the real transition name from `qualified` to `customer` - there is no `qualify_to_customer` transition in the actual spec) carries `Customer{NameField: "name", EmailField: "email", PhoneField: "phone", AddressField: "address"}`.

Four named template fields on `TransitionSpec`, not a generic `Effects []TransitionEffect` slice, is a deliberate choice, not an oversight: every one of these four bridges shares the same shape (a set of named payload-field references, one of them "required" in the skip-vs-apply sense, applied inside the same transaction), so a generic slice would mostly just move the same fields behind a `Kind`-tagged union without actually reducing the amount of code - and four named fields stay self-documenting at every call site (`stock_to_sale.go`/`customer_pipeline.go` read as "this transition does these things", not "this transition has effects, go look up what kind 2 means"). `TransitionSpec`'s own doc comment states the threshold for reconsidering this explicitly: a fifth or sixth such bridge is the signal to switch to the generic `Effects` shape, not before.

`customer_id` is a new OPTIONAL `FieldTypeCustomer` field on `stock_to_sale`'s payload (alongside the new `destination_address`/`origin_address`/`carrier`/`tracking_number` string fields the `Delivery` template above reads). When present and it resolves to a real customer, `record_sale`'s journal entry description gets that customer's name appended - `resolveJournalDescription` (`engine.go`) interpolates the base `Journal.Description` template exactly as before (so every pre-existing sale with no `customer_id` renders identically, no dangling placeholder), then, only if `customer_id` resolves via `crm.GetCustomerNameTx`, appends `" (customer: {{customer_name}})"` and re-runs the same `interpolateTemplate` mechanism against a small derived payload - the same `{{field}}` placeholder syntax every other template in this codebase already uses, composed conditionally in Go rather than baked into the static template string, specifically to avoid degrading the common (no customer) case.

- `delivery_id`/`customer_id` payload fields (well, `customer_id` is the only one currently wired onto a real spec - `FieldTypeDelivery` exists for future workflows to reference an existing delivery) are two new `FieldType`s (`spec.go`) - the same shape `FieldTypeProduct`/`FieldTypeSupplier` already established: resolves against one specific table directly via `checkDeliveryField`/`checkCustomerField` (`engine.go`) calling `logistics.DeliveryExistsTx`/`crm.CustomerExistsTx`.
- `CreateInstanceForm.tsx` renders `FieldTypeDelivery`/`FieldTypeCustomer` fields as plain `<select>`s, the same picker shape `FieldTypeProduct`/`FieldTypeSupplier`/`FieldTypePerson` already share.

**KPI additions**: `pendingDeliveries` (count of `deliveries` with `status IN ('pending', 'in_transit')`) was classified under the EXISTING `KPIKindInventoryKPI` kind rather than a new one - it's an operational fulfillment metric, the same register `lowStockProducts`/`totalInventoryValue` already occupy, not a customer-relationship metric. `activeCustomers` (count of `customers` rows with a non-null `source_workflow_instance`, i.e. actually converted from the pipeline rather than manually entered) got its own new `KPIKindCRM` kind instead, since it counts *relationships* rather than *operational state* - a genuinely different aggregation shape from every existing `Kind` (account balances, workflow-state counts, people counts, or inventory levels).

### Scope boundaries

No route optimization or map integration, no carrier API integration (tracking numbers/carriers are free-text fields, not integrations), no invoicing (a future batch combining `customers` + `journal_entries` into a formatted document), no loyalty points or customer pricing tiers.

## Invoicing, payment tracking, and receivables management

`internal/invoicing` connects the accounting, customer, and sales modules into a real receivables cycle: an invoice (`invoices`/`invoice_lines`) generated against a sale, closed by a recorded payment (`payments`) that auto-posts a real ledger entry. Invoices are created two ways: directly, owner-gated, via `CreateInvoice`; or automatically, by the workflow-to-invoicing bridge (`TransitionSpec.Invoice`, see below), via `CreateInvoiceTx`, when a transition carrying an `InvoiceTemplate` fires with a resolvable quantity and unit price. `SourceWorkflowInstance` distinguishes the latter from the former, the same role `customers.source_workflow_instance`/`deliveries.source_id` already play. `invoices`/`invoice_lines`/`invoice_sequences` are mutable (`zonaryos_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE`); `payments` is append-only (`SELECT, INSERT` only), the same immutability principle `journal_entries`/`stock_movements` already establish - a recorded payment is a historical fact, corrected by a new/reversing payment, never rewritten. `IsMember` gates every read; `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates every write.

- HTTP surface: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/invoices`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/firms/{firmID}/invoices/{invoiceID}` (`PATCH` changes status only), `POST`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/firms/{firmID}/invoices/{invoiceID}/lines[/{lineID}]`, `POST`/`GET /api/firms/{firmID}/invoices/{invoiceID}/payments`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/reports/receivables-aging`.
- Frontend: `/invoices` (`components/Invoicing/InvoicesManager.tsx`) - an invoice list with a deliberately minimal one-line create form (full line-item editing stays a backend-only capability for now, matching this codebase's "functional, not polished" first-pass convention); `/invoices/{invoiceID}` (`components/Invoicing/InvoiceDetail.tsx`) - line detail, a manual status-change control (gated by the same allowed-transitions set the backend enforces), recorded payments, and the "Record Payment" form; `/financials` gained a fourth tab, Receivables Aging.

**Sequential invoice numbering, collision-free under concurrency**: `nextInvoiceNumberTx` (`invoices.go`) allocates `INV-0001`, `INV-0002`, ... via a single UPSERT against a dedicated per-firm counter table, `invoice_sequences (firm_id PRIMARY KEY, next_number)` - not a Postgres `SEQUENCE` object (database-wide, not firm-scoped) and not `count(*) + 1` against `invoices` itself (racy under concurrent inserts, and wrong after any invoice is ever deleted): `INSERT INTO invoice_sequences (firm_id, next_number) VALUES ($1, 2) ON CONFLICT (firm_id) DO UPDATE SET next_number = invoice_sequences.next_number + 1 RETURNING next_number - 1`. The `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` arm takes a row-level lock on that firm's one counter row for the rest of the transaction - a second, concurrent call for the same firm blocks at this statement until the first transaction commits or rolls back, so it can never read the same `next_number` the first call just claimed. `TestNextInvoiceNumberTx_ConcurrentCallsGetDistinctNumbers` (`invoicing_integration_test.go`) fires 10 concurrent `CreateInvoice` calls against the same firm and asserts all 10 succeed with 10 distinct numbers.

**The payment-closes-receivables cycle**: `RecordPayment` (`payments.go`) inserts a `payments` row against an invoice, then - in the SAME transaction - checks whether that invoice's total recorded payments (computed via Postgres's own exact `numeric` `SUM`, never a Go float) now cover its own `total`. If so, it moves the invoice to `'paid'` AND posts a real `DR Cash (1000) / CR Trade Receivables (1100)` journal entry (`internal/accounting.PostJournalEntryTx`), sourced as `"invoice"`/`invoiceID` (not `"workflow_instance"`, since an invoice - not a workflow instance - is what actually closed here). Both the payment and the closing side effects commit or roll back together. A partial payment leaves the invoice exactly as it was (still whatever status it already had); only a payment that brings the running total to `>= total` triggers the close. Status can otherwise only move through a small, hand-validated state machine (`allowedStatusTransitions`, `invoices.go`) - `draft -> sent/cancelled`, `sent -> overdue/cancelled`, `overdue -> sent/cancelled` - deliberately NOT `internal/workflow`'s generic engine (the design brief's own "keep this simple, no separate workflow"): `'paid'` is reachable only through `RecordPayment`, never a manual `PATCH`.

**The receivables aging report** (`reports.go`): `ReceivablesAging` groups every unpaid invoice (`status IN ('sent', 'overdue')`) into one of four fixed buckets - `0-30`/`31-60`/`61-90`/`90+` - by `CURRENT_DATE - COALESCE(due_date, issued_date)`, computed entirely in SQL so the report is always "as of right now," not a value that could go stale between the query and the response being formatted:

```sql
WITH aged AS (
    SELECT total, (CURRENT_DATE - COALESCE(due_date, issued_date)) AS days_overdue
    FROM invoices WHERE status IN ('sent', 'overdue')
),
bucketed AS (
    SELECT CASE
        WHEN days_overdue <= 30 THEN '0-30'
        WHEN days_overdue <= 60 THEN '31-60'
        WHEN days_overdue <= 90 THEN '61-90'
        ELSE '90+'
    END AS label, total
    FROM aged
)
SELECT label, count(*), COALESCE(SUM(total), 0)::text FROM bucketed GROUP BY label
```

Every one of the four labels always appears in the response (`count=0`/`outstanding="0.0000"` if nothing fell into it), the same "every key always appears, zero when empty" convention `internal/reports.computeKPI`'s own callers already rely on.

**The workflow-to-invoicing bridge and the fifth-template design tension**: `TransitionSpec` (`spec.go`) gained a fifth optional template, `Invoice *InvoiceTemplate` - the exact same shape (a `*XTemplate` pointer, resolved by `ExecuteTransition` against the merged instance payload, applied through `internal/invoicing.CreateInvoiceTx` in the SAME transaction as the state change) `Journal`/`StockAdjustment`/`Delivery`/`Customer` already established. This is deliberately flagged, not silently absorbed: `TransitionSpec`'s own doc comment, written during the Logistics/CRM batch, states "a fifth or sixth such bridge... is the signal to switch to the generic `Effects` shape - not before." Invoice genuinely is that fifth bridge. Rather than either quietly ignoring the self-imposed threshold or undertaking an unplanned refactor of four already-shipped, already-tested bridges mid-batch, the doc comment was updated to record the trade-off explicitly: `Invoice` ships as a fifth named field now (keeping this batch's actual scope - invoicing - the focus), with an explicit line drawn under it - the next bridge added the same way, without first doing the `Effects`-slice refactor, is the real overreach. `stock_to_sale`'s `record_sale` transition carries `Invoice{Description: "Sale of {{item}}", ProductField: "product_id", QuantityField: "quantity", UnitPriceField: "unit_price", CustomerField: "customer_id"}` - `CustomerField` reuses the existing `customer_id` payload field (already read by the `Delivery` template's own journal-description feature), not a new one. `QuantityField`/`UnitPriceField` are both required for the whole template to apply (Rule 6: every pre-existing `record_sale` call with no price data keeps working, no invoice created); `ProductField`/`CustomerField` are optional and simply leave the corresponding column `NULL`.

### Scope boundaries

No PDF generation (invoices as structured data first, a future batch), no recurring invoices, no credit notes/refunds, no multi-currency invoice settlement, no customer payment portal.

### Running these tests

`internal/invoicing/invoicing_integration_test.go` (invoice/line/payment CRUD, the sequential-numbering concurrency proof, the payment-closes-receivables journal entry, the aging report's own bucket correctness), `internal/workflow/invoicing_bridge_integration_test.go` (the `record_sale` invoice-creation hook end-to-end, including the customer-field reuse), and `internal/reports/kpi_integration_test.go` (the two new receivables KPIs' correctness) all need a real Postgres:

```

export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable

## Parametric reporting engine, document templates, and CSV export

Extends "HR core... and the reporting foundation" above: `/reports` no longer serves only the five fixed `kpiDescriptors` - firms can now define their own reports as structured query descriptors, never raw SQL.

**`internal/reports`'s parametric engine**: two new firm-scoped tables, `report_definitions` (`query_spec` jsonb) and `saved_report_runs` (one row per execution, `status`/`result`/`error_text`). `QuerySpec` (`queryspec.go`) is `{entity, filters, group_by, metrics, date_range}` - `entity`/every field name/every aggregation is validated against `entityRegistry`, a closed, hardcoded per-entity allow-list (table + column + type per field, covering `workflow_instances`/`journal_entries`/`invoices`/`deliveries`/`people`/`products`). `BuildQuery` is the ONLY place a `QuerySpec` becomes SQL: every identifier in the generated query comes from `entityRegistry` (never from the request), every value is bound via Postgres `$N` parameters (never interpolated) - an entity/field/aggregation not on the allow-list is rejected with `ErrInvalidQuerySpec` (HTTP 400) before any SQL is built, which is what rules out both SQL injection and information disclosure through a made-up field name. `filter.op` reuses `internal/workflow`'s own `eq/neq/lt/gt/lte/gte/contains` vocabulary. `RunReport` executes the built query synchronously and records the run in `saved_report_runs`.

- HTTP surface: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/report-definitions`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/report-definitions/{id}`, `POST /api/firms/{firmID}/report-definitions/{id}/run`.
- Frontend: `/reports/builder` (`components/Reports/ReportBuilder.tsx` - pick entity/filters/grouping/metrics/date range, "Save & Run" shows results inline) and a "My Reports" tab on `/reports` (`components/Reports/MyReportsPanel.tsx` - list saved definitions, run one, see results).

**`internal/documents`** (new package): firm-scoped `document_templates` (`type` one of `invoice`/`delivery_note`/`report`, `template` a Handlebars/Mustache-*looking* `{{field}}` body) rendered to HTML via Go's stdlib `html/template` (no new dependency) - PDF generation stays future work, same "structured first, formatted later" philosophy `internal/invoicing`'s own doc comment already establishes. `RenderInvoice` builds the template's data context as a nested `map[string]any` (invoice + lines + customer + firm info) with `template.Option("missingkey=zero")` set, so a template referencing a field that doesn't exist on the entity renders an empty string rather than failing the whole render. A minimal default invoice template is seeded per firm lazily, the first time `ListDocumentTemplates`/`RenderInvoice` runs for a firm with none yet (`EnsureDefaultInvoiceTemplate`) - there's no Go `init()` hook with a firm ID to seed against, so this is done on first use instead of at firm-creation time.

- HTTP surface: `GET`/`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/document-templates`, `GET`/`PUT /api/firms/{firmID}/document-templates/{id}`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/invoices/{invoiceID}/render?templateID=` (returns `Content-Type: text/html`, no `templateID` falls back to the firm's default invoice template).
- Frontend: `/settings/document-templates` (`components/Settings/DocumentTemplatesManager.tsx` - a textarea editor, not a visual builder) and a "Preview" link on the invoice detail page that opens the rendered HTML in a new tab via a same-origin, cookie-authenticated proxy route (`app/api/documents/invoices/[firmId]/[invoiceId]/render`).

**CSV export**: `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/export` now supports format negotiation via `?format=csv` or `Accept: text/csv` (default stays `json`, unchanged) - `internal/portability.BuildCSV` renders each `ExportDocument` entity as its own CSV section (blank line + entity-name header, via stdlib `encoding/csv`, no new dependency), field names/values drawn entirely from `ExportDocument`'s own Go struct definitions via reflection, not request input. CSV export is read-only - import remains JSON-only (see `internal/portability`'s own doc comment on why import only ever reads configuration, not history).

### Scope boundaries

No visual report builder with drag-and-drop, no real-time/streaming report execution, no scheduled report delivery. No PDF generation (HTML only). No CSV import (export only).

### Running these tests

`internal/reports/queryspec_test.go` (unit: `BuildQuery` correctness, unknown entity/field/op rejection, including the explicit malicious-field-name SQL-injection-surface cases), `internal/reports/definitions_integration_test.go` (definition CRUD + `RunReport` correctness against real seeded invoices), `internal/documents/render_test.go` (unit: template rendering, the unknown-field-renders-empty contract), `internal/documents/documents_integration_test.go` (template CRUD, default-template seeding, `RenderInvoice` end to end), and `internal/portability/csv_test.go` (unit: CSV section structure) - only the `_integration_test.go` files need a real Postgres:

```

export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable

## Multi-tenant isolation hardening, performance, and cross-module linking

Four parts, no new modules - an audit/hardening/linking batch over what already exists.

**Part 1, N+1 query audit**: scanned every `List*` function for a fetch-then-loop pattern. Two real N+1s found and fixed, both in `internal/portability.ExportFirm`: it looped `inventory.GetStock` once per product (fixed by a new `inventory.ListStockForFirm`, one query for the whole firm) and looped `invoicing.GetInvoice` once per invoice to pull lines (fixed by a new `invoicing.ListInvoicesWithLines`, which batches every invoice's lines in with one extra `WHERE invoice_id = ANY($1)` query - the same two-query shape `accounting.ListJournalEntries` already uses for `journal_lines`). One N+1 found and deliberately left: `workflow.ExportDefinitionSpecs` (used only by `ExportFirm`, so bounded by a firm's own definition count, not a hot read path) does two extra round trips per workflow definition (states, then transitions) - already documented in-code as a pgx constraint (can't interleave `Query()` calls on the same open `Rows`), and not worth the added complexity of batching for an owner-only, occasional export operation. `internal/platform/db/dbtest.ExplainAnalyze(t, pool, query, args...)` is a new test-only helper (a separate package so `testing` never becomes a production dependency) for ad hoc `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, FORMAT TEXT)` investigation - not run by any test automatically.

**Part 2, performance indexes** (`migrations/0022_performance_indexes.up.sql`, additive-only, `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, not `CONCURRENTLY` - no multi-instance deployment target yet, item 34): `workflow_instances(firm_id, workflow_definition_id)`, `invoices(source_workflow_instance)` (a real pre-existing gap - `customers.source_workflow_instance` already had one, `invoices`' own didn't, and Part 3a's new lookups need it), `edge_events(firm_id, agent_id, received_at)`, `stock_movements(firm_id, product_id, created_at)`. `journal_lines(entry_id)`, `invoice_lines(invoice_id)`, and `notifications`/`scheduled_rule_runs`' own partial indexes already covered the rest of the design brief's list from earlier migrations - not duplicated.

**Part 3, cross-module linking** - three read-only views connecting existing data that was already stored but never surfaced:
- 3a: `invoicing.ListInvoicesBySourceInstance` + `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/workflow-instances/{instanceID}/invoices` (registered in `internal/workflow`, which already imports `internal/invoicing` for the invoicing bridge). A new minimal workflow-instance detail page, `/workflows/instance/[instanceId]`, shows state/payload plus "Associated invoices" - the destination for the invoice detail page's own new "Source" link, and for `WorkflowInstanceList.tsx`'s new "View" link per row.
- 3b: the `/logistics` page fetches each `workflow_instance`-sourced delivery's own associated invoice server-side (via 3a's endpoint) and links to it directly (falling back to the workflow-instance detail page if no invoice exists yet).
- 3c: `workflow.ListInstancesByCustomer` + `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/workflow-instances?customerId=` (a required query param on the bare collection path, not a nested path segment - the same wildcard-vs-literal `ServeMux` ambiguity this file's own `?key=` comment above already describes, since `/workflow-instances/by-customer/{x}` and `/workflow-instances/{instanceID}/invoices` can't be told apart when `{x}` could be `"invoices"`). Filters `workflow_instances.payload ->> 'customer_id' = $2` across every definition (not hardcoded to `stock_to_sale`, though that's the only one populating it today). A new `/customers/[customerId]` detail page shows the source lead link (`customer.sourceWorkflowInstance`, already exposed) and the "Sales" list - "the first real CRM view that connects sales to customers".

**Part 4, bulk operations** - both new endpoints reuse an existing single-entity function's core logic, refactored to take an already-open `pgx.Tx` instead of opening its own, so a bulk call can share ONE transaction across every entity (true atomicity: any failure rolls back everything already done in that same call, via `zdb.WithFirmContext`'s own `defer tx.Rollback(ctx)`) rather than each entity committing independently:
- `workflow.ExecuteTransition`'s entire body became `executeTransitionTx` (unexported, takes `tx pgx.Tx`); `ExecuteTransition` itself is now a thin pool-opening wrapper. `workflow.BulkExecuteTransition` (`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/workflow-instances/bulk-transition`) checks every instance shares one definition (`ErrBulkTransitionMixedDefinitions`) before touching any of them, then calls `executeTransitionTx` per instance inside one transaction - each instance's own compatible-state and permission checks happen exactly as they do for a single transition. Unlike `ExecuteTransition`, it does NOT run `EvaluateRules` per instance after commit (deferred scope - see this batch's own "no async job execution" boundary).
- `invoicing.UpdateInvoiceStatus`'s body became `updateInvoiceStatusTx`; `invoicing.BulkUpdateInvoiceStatus` (`POST /api/firms/{firmID}/invoices/bulk-status`, owner-only) loops it inside one transaction the same way.

No frontend for bulk operations yet (API only, per the design brief).

### Scope boundaries

No query caching layer (Redis, etc.), no read replica routing, no async job execution for long-running reports, no bulk delete.

### Running these tests

`internal/invoicing/n1_integration_test.go` (a query-counting `pgx.QueryTracer` proves `ListInvoicesWithLines` makes a FIXED number of round trips regardless of invoice count - Part 1), `internal/invoicing/source_instance_integration_test.go` and `internal/workflow/customer_links_integration_test.go` (Part 3's cross-link correctness), `internal/workflow/bulk_transition_integration_test.go` and `internal/invoicing/bulk_status_integration_test.go` (Part 4's atomicity - a partial failure leaves every other entity in the batch untouched) all need a real Postgres:

```

export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
make migrate
go test ./internal/invoicing/... -v
go test ./internal/workflow/... -run 'ByCustomer|BulkExecuteTransition' -v
```

## Edge Agent protocol foundation

`internal/edgeagent` is the server-side half of Vision §9's Edge Agent (a Go process a firm runs on its own hardware, under a firm-specific account, with auto-update and a bidirectional bridge to the central server). This batch ships the protocol only - see "Scope boundaries" below for what's deliberately deferred.

Three firm-scoped tables (`migrations/0018_edge_agent_protocol.up.sql`), mandatory RLS like every other table in this codebase: `edge_agents` (one row per registered agent, `status` a closed `offline`/`online`/`error` vocabulary), `edge_agent_tokens` (bearer-token credentials, storing only a SHA-256 `token_hash` - the plaintext is returned once, at issuance, and never persisted), `edge_events` (an append-only log of whatever the agent chooses to push, `event_type` + a `jsonb` `payload`).

**Two entirely separate authentication paths.** Registering an agent, issuing/listing tokens, and listing events go through the ordinary Keycloak bearer-token path (`internal/identity.Middleware`) exactly like every other module - `IsMember` gates reads, `IsMember` then `IsOwner` gates writes, same tier as `internal/logistics`'s own deliveries. The agent itself has no Keycloak account: `POST /api/edge/heartbeat` and `POST /api/edge/events` authenticate purely via the bearer token `IssueToken` handed it once.

**Token authentication design**: `IssueToken` (`tokens.go`) generates 32 random bytes, base64url-encodes them (with a cosmetic `eat_` prefix), and persists only the SHA-256 hex digest as `token_hash` - the plaintext is returned to the caller exactly once, in `IssueToken`'s own return value, and this codebase never has it again. On the request path, `withTokenTx` hashes the presented token and looks it up by indexed hash equality against `edge_agent_tokens.token_hash` (safe: equality timing on a digest of a 32-byte random secret leaks nothing about the plaintext, the same "compare hashes, not secrets" reasoning production API-key schemes rely on), then applies `crypto/subtle.ConstantTimeCompare` between the fetched and recomputed hash bytes as an explicit, literal constant-time comparison before trusting the row. **`firm_id` binding**: this lookup happens through a new RLS carve-out, `app_current_edge_token_hash()` (mirroring `app_current_invite_token()` from `migrations/0007_firm_invites.up.sql` exactly) - the token authenticates itself first, and only once its real `firm_id` is known does `withTokenTx` set `app.current_firm_id` for the rest of the transaction, the same two-step sequencing `db.WithFirmContext`'s own doc comment attributes to `internal/wizard.CreateDefaultFirm`/`internal/invite.Accept`. Crucially, `Heartbeat`/`RecordEvent` take no client-supplied `firmID`/`agentID` at all - the agent's identity comes entirely from the validated token (`AuthenticatedAgent{FirmID, AgentID}`), so there is no field for a caller to guess or spoof to reach another firm's data; `TestCrossFirmToken_CannotAccessAnotherFirmsAgent` (`edgeagent_integration_test.go`) is the regression proof.

- HTTP surface: `POST`/`GET /api/firms/{firmID}/edge-agents`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/edge-agents/{agentID}`, `POST`/`GET /api/firms/{firmID}/edge-agents/{agentID}/tokens`, `GET /api/firms/{firmID}/edge-agents/{agentID}/events` (all Keycloak-authenticated); `POST /api/edge/heartbeat`, `POST /api/edge/events` (token-authenticated, no firm/agent path segment at all).
- Frontend: `/edge-agents` (`components/EdgeAgents/EdgeAgentsManager.tsx`) - agent list with a status dot, last-seen, and an owner-gated registration form; `/edge-agents/{agentID}` (`components/EdgeAgents/EdgeAgentDetail.tsx`) - status/capabilities, the event log, and owner-only token management (issuing a token shows the plaintext exactly once with an explicit "copy it now" warning; the token list below it only ever shows name/expiry/last-used, never the secret again).

### Scope boundaries

No Edge Agent binary (the actual Go process that runs on a firm's own hardware) - that's a separate deployable, out of this repository. No WebSocket/long-poll server-initiated push - the agent polls; push is a future concern. No NATS JetStream offline-resilience layer (`docs/OPEN_POINTS.md` item 29 - a separate design). No OTA update mechanism.

### Running these tests

`internal/edgeagent/edgeagent_integration_test.go` (owner-gated agent creation, token issuance hashing/one-time-plaintext, heartbeat status/`last_seen_at` updates, and the cross-firm token rejection regression) needs a real Postgres:

```
export ZONARYOS_TEST_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos:zonaryos@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
make migrate
go test ./internal/edgeagent/... -v
```

## Observability: structured logging and health checks

**Structured logging**: every operational log line this repository's binaries emit (`cmd/server`, `cmd/auditpurge`, `cmd/migrate`, `cmd/licensegen`, plus `internal/workflow`'s rule-evaluation warning and `internal/telemetry`'s flush logging) goes through `log/slog` (stdlib, no new dependency) rather than the plain-text `log` package, each binary's `main` wiring `slog.SetDefault(slog.New(slog.NewJSONHandler(os.Stdout, nil)))` (`cmd/licensegen` uses `os.Stderr` instead, since its plaintext token output on stdout must stay uninterleaved with log lines) so every line is directly machine-parseable JSON. This is deliberately separate from `internal/auditlog`, which keeps recording business events (who did what, in which firm) to Postgres exactly as before - `slog` is for operational events (startup, request errors, unexpected states), never a replacement for the audit trail.

**`GET /health`** (`internal/health`, unauthenticated by design - same posture as `GET /healthz` and `GET /.well-known/zonaryos-central`, since an operator or uptime monitor needs to check this before any credential exists to present): checks Postgres (a lightweight `pool.Ping`) and Keycloak (a GET against its own OIDC discovery document, the same one `internal/identity.NewVerifier` fetches at startup) **in parallel** via `sync.WaitGroup`, each capped at a 5s timeout - run sequentially instead, a wedged dependency checked first would cost up to 2x that ceiling instead of the ~1x this endpoint actually promises. Responds:

```json
{
  "status": "ok" | "degraded" | "error",
  "checks": { "database": "ok" | "error", "keycloak": "ok" | "error" },
  "version": "..."
}
```

`status` is `"ok"` only if every check passed, `"error"` only if every check failed, `"degraded"` otherwise (a non-2xx HTTP status, `503`, is used for anything but `"ok"`). This is distinct from the pre-existing `GET /healthz` (`internal/platform/httpapi`), which only ever reports the process itself is up and answering HTTP - a liveness probe, not a dependency check. `version` comes from `internal/platform/version.Version`, a package-level `var` defaulting to `"dev"`, overridable at build time via `-ldflags "-X .../internal/platform/version.Version=<value>"` once a real build/release pipeline exists (`docs/OPEN_POINTS.md` item 34 - none does yet).

**`GET /metrics`**: a stub only, same default-closed posture `internal/license`'s own surfaces establish - it unconditionally returns `401 Unauthorized`, deliberately never exposing real metrics (request latencies, counts, etc. - no metrics backend is wired up yet) to an unauthenticated caller. Documented here rather than implemented further so a future batch that does wire up real metrics has an explicit "this was always meant to require auth" marker to work from, not a silent surprise.

### Running the health package tests

`internal/health/health_test.go` is dependency-free (a nil pool, plus a `httptest.Server` standing in for Keycloak's discovery endpoint) and runs as an ordinary unit test, no Postgres required:

```
go test ./internal/health/... -v
```

## Firm-creation wizard

`internal/wizard` implements Vision §3's "Self-Configuring Infrastructure": a newly-authenticated Keycloak user with zero firm memberships (detected via `internal/identity.Memberships`, PR 3's firm-discovery mechanism) is routed into this wizard instead of the normal app.

- **`tree.go`** models the wizard as an actual decision tree, not a hardcoded form: a `Node` is either a question (with `Answers`, each pointing at the next `Node`), a terminal action, or a terminal placeholder. This batch (Open Points item 12) replaced the original single-question slice (`"do you manufacture?"` only) with the tree's real root question sequence, ordered per Vision §3's principle ("questions that unlock features almost every commercial business needs come first"): (1) `doYouSellProductsOrServices` - `"yes"` opens two sub-questions, `doYouTrackPhysicalInventory` and `doYouManageCustomerRelationships`, before continuing; `"no"` skips straight to question 2; (2) `doYouPurchaseFromSuppliers`, asked either way; (3) `doYouManageTasksOrApprovals`, asked either way (tasks/approvals are universal, not conditioned on selling); (4) `doYouManagePeople` (payroll/time-tracking/absence batch) - "do you have employees or people you manage?", asked either way, right before the manufacturing question; (5) `doYouManufacture` - `"no"` reaches the `create_default_firm` action, `"yes"` still dead-ends at the pre-existing `manufacturingComingSoon` placeholder (manufacturing support itself remains out of scope). `Node` fields carry i18n message keys (not literal text) for anything user-facing, matching Never-Violate Rule 4. `scripts/e2e_smoke_test.sh`'s `wizard_walk` call sites all carry one extra `yes`/`no` answer for this question as of this batch - update every call site there if the question order ever changes again.
- **`SeedSelection`** (`tree.go`) is the accumulated yes/no answers from every workflow-gating root question (`Sells`/`TracksInventory`/`ManagesCRM`/`PurchasesFromSuppliers`/`ManagesTasks`/`ManagesPeople`). This wizard's HTTP surface is stateless request/response per node - there is no session carrying prior answers forward - so `SeedSelection` is NOT client-supplied round-tripped state: `buildTree` constructs every distinct path from `RootNode` to its own terminal `Node`, each carrying a `*SeedSelection` literal baked in at tree-construction time (`Node.Seed`, only set on the `create_default_firm` action node). The path taken to reach a terminal node *is* the record of every answer that led there.
- **`firm.go`**'s `CreateDefaultFirm` is the wizard's one implemented action, now parametric on `SeedSelection` (Open Points item 37's resolution: "Parametric New-Firm Workflow Seeding") instead of unconditionally seeding the same hardcoded pair for every firm: it creates the firm, a default `owner` role, the caller's membership in it, seeds the firm's starter chart of accounts (`accounting.SeedDefaultChartOfAccountsTx`, passing `ManagesPeople: sel.ManagesPeople` - see the "Financial management core" section above), then seeds `workflow.StockToSaleSpec` only if `Sells && TracksInventory`, `workflow.CustomerPipelineSpec` only if `Sells && ManagesCRM`, `workflow.PurchaseOrderSpec` only if `PurchasesFromSuppliers`, `workflow.TaskApprovalSpec` only if `ManagesTasks`, and `workflow.AbsenceApprovalSpec` only if `ManagesPeople` (see the "Payroll, time tracking, and absence management" section below) - via `workflow.SeedStockToSaleWorkflowTx`/`SeedCustomerPipelineWorkflowTx`/`SeedPurchaseOrderWorkflowTx`/`SeedTaskApprovalWorkflowTx`/`SeedAbsenceApprovalWorkflowTx` (each reused as-is), all inside one hand-managed transaction. A firm whose answers ask for none of these still gets created, with zero seeded workflow definitions - the owner defines their own from scratch via `DefineWorkflowForFirm`, per item 37's resolution. `internal/platform/db.WithFirmContext` can't be used here since it needs a `firmID` up front and the firm doesn't exist yet; this function sets `app.current_firm_id` itself, once the new firm's ID is known, before touching any RLS-protected table - the write-side counterpart to the read-side bootstrap `migrations/0002_user_scoped_discovery.up.sql` solved for firm discovery. One `audit_log` row is written for the firm's creation, same table/shape the workflow engine already writes to.
- **`purchase_order.go`**/**`task_approval.go`** (`internal/workflow`) are this batch's two new minimal starter workflows, seeded only when the wizard's corresponding question is answered "yes" (see above): **Purchase Order** is `draft` (initial) -> `sent` -> `received`/`cancelled` (both terminal, only reachable from `sent`) - see the "Financial management core" section above for its `receive`-only journal template and its `total_amount`/`supplier_name` payload fields; **Task / Approval** is `open` (initial) -> `in_progress` -> `done`/`rejected` (both terminal, only reachable from `in_progress`) - see the "HR core" section below for its `assignee_person_id` payload field (a later batch's addition, `FieldTypePerson`).
- **Default role's permissions**: for each workflow `CreateDefaultFirm` actually seeds, it passes the new `owner` role's ID into that workflow's `SeedWorkflowTx` function, so `DefineWorkflowTx`'s self-action auto-grant (see the workflow engine section above) grants that role every permission the seeded workflow introduces - `CreateDefaultFirm` itself has no bespoke grant step. There is nobody else yet to grant permissions to the founder, so "everything this firm's own provisioning has introduced so far" is the deliberate default, not a restricted starter role - and it's not a superuser bypass either: see `migrations/0004_role_owner_flag.up.sql`'s `is_owner` column, which marks the role for Permission Audit Mode's future UI (Vision §3: "roles at the highest permission tier ... don't appear in these lists") but is never consulted by `permission.Has` or any other check.
- HTTP surface (not scoped under `/api/firms/{firmID}/...` - no firm exists yet, same bootstrap category as `/api/me`): `GET /api/wizard/nodes/{nodeKey}` reads a node; `POST /api/wizard/nodes/{nodeKey}/answer` submits an answer and, if it resolves to `create_default_firm`, creates the firm inline (using that path's baked-in `SeedSelection`) and returns it as `result` - see `docs/api/openapi.yaml`. The frontend's `WizardClient.tsx` collects the firm name once, up front, and carries it through every answer submitted from then on (rather than only validating it on a hardcoded `"no"` answer, which was specific to the old single-question tree) - any question could now turn out to be the tree's last one.

### Running the wizard tests

`internal/wizard/tree_test.go` is a pure unit test (tree traversal/lookup - including the full multi-question path and each answer's accumulated `SeedSelection`, no database). `internal/wizard/firm_integration_test.go` needs a real Postgres, same convention as the workflow engine tests above; `TestSeedSelection_SeedsExactlyExpectedWorkflows` there is Open Points item 37's concrete proof - a table of every yes/no combination, asserting `CreateDefaultFirm` seeds exactly the workflows implied and no others:

```
export ZONARYOS_TEST_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos:zonaryos@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
export ZONARYOS_TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL=postgres://zonaryos_app:zonaryos_app@localhost:5433/zonaryos?sslmode=disable
make migrate
go test ./internal/wizard/... -v
```

## Firm renaming and metadata

`internal/firm` started as the narrowest possible package - one function, `UpdateName`, renaming a firm - and stayed narrow, but now also covers Open Points item 36's broader firm-metadata fields: `address`, `tax_id`, `default_locale`, `default_currency`, `logo_url` (`migrations/0006_firm_metadata.up.sql`, additive-only - five new nullable columns, no change to any existing one). Still deliberately not a general, extensible firm-settings module: the `firms` table's jsonb `attributes` column still has no mutation path anywhere, and item 36's own open questions (what `attributes` should hold, whether firm settings should eventually become a real versioned module) remain unresolved - see the Open Points entry below.

- **`Update`** (name required, `UpdateFields` optional) trims and rejects an empty name (`ErrInvalidName`) before touching the database - same "validate before opening a transaction" shape `wizard.CreateDefaultFirm` already uses for `firmName`. Each metadata field in `UpdateFields` is a `*string`: `nil` leaves that column alone, a pointer to `""` clears it back to `NULL`, a pointer to a non-empty value sets it (after trimming and a basic sanity length cap - `address`/`taxId` 500/100 chars, `defaultLocale`/`defaultCurrency` 20/10 chars, `logoUrl` 2048 chars - rejected with `ErrMetadataTooLong` if exceeded; this is garbage-input protection, not fiscal-format validation, item 24 stays open). Owner-gated (`IsMember` then `IsOwner`), same tier as defining a new workflow. Writes one `audit_log` row (`entity_type` `"firm"`, `action` `"update"`) recording exactly which fields changed - same table/shape `CreateDefaultFirm`'s own firm-creation entry uses, just a different action value. `UpdateName(ctx, pool, firmID, userID, name)` is kept as a thin wrapper over `Update` with a zero-value `UpdateFields`, for existing callers/tests that only ever touch the name.
- **`Get`** reads a firm's current name and metadata, gated by plain `IsMember` rather than `IsOwner` - viewing a firm's own basic info doesn't need the stricter tier that editing it does.
